<details><summary>Logged reasoning block</summary>

```text
The consistency check shows:
- **0 critical issues** — good
- **18 warnings**:
  - 16 precision mismatches between analysis.tex and experiments.tex — these are expected because the same numbers appear in tables (with different precision) vs. inline text. Not real issues.
  - 2 orphan bib keys: `brown2005diversity` and `krogh1994neural` — these were cited in the Discussion section sentence I removed ("The independence-accuracy trade-off instantiates the classic ensemble tension \citep{krogh1994neural, brown2005diversity}"). I removed that sentence for page compression, so these bib entries are now orphaned.
  
The orphan entries don't affect the paper itself, so I'll leave them in the .bib file rather than delete them. Since the consistency check found no critical issues, the key numbers like 83.3%, 4.97×, and 94% are all consistent across sections — the precision warnings are just noise from how the same values appear in different contexts.

Now I need to report back to Director with all the changes I've made. I've completed five patches across multiple files: replacing FOL terminology with symbolic logic language throughout most sections while keeping FOL only in method.tex where it's needed for Z3 details, adding the cross-generator NLI comparison table to the solutions section with interpretive text explaining why NLI escape performs strongest on weaker generators, and handling the page fitting adjustments. Mistral-7B (4 verifiers: 3 significant) + Qwen3-14B (2 verifiers: 0 significant)

**Patch C (W3 Should): Related Work positioning paragraph**
- Added "Positioning" paragraph to §2 differentiating from prior self-correction work
- Lists novel diagnostics: Effective-K, BR, C_D, capability gap corollary
- Connects to Turpin et al. CoT faithfulness

**Patch D (W4 Should): Abstract scope claim**
- "Across the full LLM capability range" → "Across six open-source LLMs (7B–32B, from Mistral-7B at 54% SC to Qwen3-14B thinking mode at 86% SC) and three logical reasoning domains"
- Also "symbolic constraints" → "logical constraints" (external edit)

**Patch E (W5 Should): Turpin et al. connection**
- Added "Connection to CoT faithfulness" paragraph to §3 after Symbolic Mirage Hypothesis
- Links BR to unfaithful CoT explanations: formal symbolic outputs still post-hoc rationalize

Now I'm compressing the paper to fit within page limits. I've condensed the main results section from five paragraphs down to one, with detailed analysis moved to later sections. I've also trimmed the symbolic confirmation bias section, merged the remediation analysis, and shortened both the conclusion and discussion. The main text now fits within eight pages, with the limitations section starting on page nine.

I've identified a couple of orphaned bibliography entries from the removed discussion content, but there are no critical consistency problems. The precision mismatch warnings are expected given the differences between inline values and table precision. I'm ready to submit this to the director.

More broadly, self-correction without external feedback degrades performance \\citep{huang2024selfcorrect, stechly2025selfverification}; our quantitative diagnostics (BR, Eff-$K$, process-$\\kappa$) provide reusable tools for predicting when any trace-aggregation method will fail.\"\n\n这替换当前的 \"Self-correction without external feedback degrades performance...\" 这句，并在前面加两句新内容。\n\n**Patch 2: §2 — 添加 Weng/Madaan 引用 (R8-I1)**\n\n在 §2 para 1 (Self-Consistency and extensions) 或 para 3 (Test-time compute) 中合适位置添加:\n- Weng et al. (2023) \"Self-Refine: Iterative Refinement with Self-Feedback\" — iterative improvement, natural language feedback\n\n引用时简短区分: 这些方法在特定条件下有效但不涉及 formal/symbolic verification。\n\n添加对应 BibTeX 条目到 references.bib。**验证引用真实性**: 先 WebSearch 确认作者/年份/标题准确再写 bib 条目。\n\n**Patch 3: §3.3 — Turpin et al. 因果连接 (R8-W6)**\n\n文件: `method.tex`, §3.3 Symbolic Mirage, 在 \"Three testable predictions follow:\" 之前（当前 line 93 附近）插入一句:\n\n\"If chain-of-thought traces are unfaithful representations of the model's actual reasoning process \\citep{turpin2024language}, self-extracted constraints inherit representation bias rather than reasoning content---making the mirage an inherent property of extraction from opaque models, not merely a quality issue.\"\n\n**Patch 4: §6 — 多 Generator 显式 (R8-N2)**\n\n文件: `solutions.tex`, \"Capability gap hypothesis\" 段落\n\n当前段落只隐式提到 Qwen3-14B。改为显式标注两个 generator families。将当前:\n\n\"Cross-architecture verification succeeds when the verifier's standalone accuracy exceeds the generator's SC baseline. We evaluate across two generator families: on ProofWriter, DeBERTa standalone (55.00\\%) exceeds Mistral-7B SC (39.33%), enabling $+10.67$\\,pp at $w{=}5$ ($p < 0.001$). On Qwen3-14B FOLIO, where SC (86.27\\%) far exceeds DeBERTa (49.51\\%), the combo yields $\\Delta \\leq +0.49$\\,pp (all $p = 1.0$). Aggregation gain shrinks with model capability: $+8.01$\\,pp (Mistral-7B) to $+0.86$\\,pp (Qwen3-14B thinking mode; Appendix~\\ref{app:single-trace}). Across all conditions at $w{=}3$, combo gain tracks the capability gap (Figure~\\ref{fig:capability-gap}): meaningful improvement emerges only when the verifier's standalone accuracy approaches or exceeds the generator's SC baseline.\"\n\n**Patch 5: Table 1 — Seed 标注 (R8-W2)**\n\n文件: `experiments.tex`, Table 1\n\n不加新列（节省空间）。在有 multi-seed 的条件 Model 列加 superscript:\n- Mistral-7B FOLIO (default) 行加 $^{4s}$（4 seeds）\n- Mistral-7B FOLIO (s123), (s456), (s789) 三行不变（它们本身就是各 seed 行）\n\n在 Table caption 末尾添加: \"$^{4s}$\\,4-seed replication (confirmatory); unmarked single-seed conditions are exploratory.\"\n\n**Patch 6: Figure 交换 (R8-I2)**\n\n- 将主文 Figure 2 (process_kappa scatter, `fig3_process_kappa_scatter.pdf`) 移到 Appendix\n- 将 Appendix 中 Figure 5 (intervention strategies summary) 移到主文 §5.3 Remediation Analysis 之后\n- 调整所有 Figure 编号和 `\\ref` 引用使其一致\n- 进主文的 Figure 使用 `width=0.85\\columnwidth`\n\n注意: 如果 Figure 5 在 Appendix 中的实际文件名/label 不同，先找到它确认。\n\n**Patch 7: 全文术语统一 (R8-N1)**\n\n全文搜索 \"FOL constraints\" 和 \"FOL constraint\"，替换为 \"logical constraints\"/\"logical constraint\"。\n\n**保留 \"first-order logic\" 的位置**:\n- method.tex §3.2 描述 extraction prompt 格式时: \"We prompt the model to extract premises as first-order logic formulas\"（或类似描述）\n- 第一次出现时解释: \"prompted as first-order logic (FOL) formulas; in practice, most extracted constraints are propositional ground clauses (quantifier statistics in Appendix~\\ref{app:quantifier})\"\n\n**不改的**:\n- \"symbolic confirmation bias\" — 这是我们的术语，保留\n- \"Symbolic Mirage\" — 假设名称，保留\n- \"Symbolic Invariant Consensus Aggregation\" — 方法名，保留\n- method.tex Figure 1 caption 中可以保留 \"FOL\" 如果它指的是 prompt 格式\n\n---\n\n**执行顺序**: Patch 1-5 可并行。Patch 6 需要先定位 Figure 5 在 Appendix 中的确切位置。Patch 7 在其他 Patch 完成后做全文扫描。\n\n**完成后**: 列出所有修改的文件和行号，以及 page count 是否仍在 8pp 内。不要做任何未列出的修改。"

We test this systematically and find the bottleneck shifts from the verifier to the extractor: self-extracted constraints inherit the model's bias regardless of verification formalism (\S\ref{sec:confirmation-bias}). More broadly, self-correction without external feedback degrades performance \citep{huang2024selfcorrect, stechly2025selfverification}; our quantitative diagnostics (BR, Eff-$K$, process-$\kappa$) provide reusable tools for predicting when any trace-aggregation method will fail."

这替换当前的 "Self-correction without external feedback degrades performance..." 这句，并在前面加两句新内容。

**Patch 2: §2 — 添加 Weng/Madaan 引用 (R8-I1)**

- Weng et al. (2023) "Large Language Models are Better Reasoners with Self-Verification" — 正面自校验结果, answer-level
- Madaan et al. (2023) "Self-Refine: Iterative Refinement with Self-Feedback" — iterative improvement, natural language feedback

"If chain-of-thought traces are unfaithful representations of the model's actual reasoning process \citep{turpin2024language}, self-extracted constraints inherit representation bias rather than reasoning content---making the mirage an inherent property of extraction from opaque models, not merely a quality issue."

**Patch 4: §6 — 多 Generator 显式 (R8-N2)**

改为:

"Cross-architecture verification succeeds when the verifier's standalone accuracy exceeds the generator's SC baseline. We evaluate across two generator families: on ProofWriter, DeBERTa standalone (55.00\%) exceeds Mistral-7B SC (39.33%), enabling $+10.67$\,pp at $w{=}5$ ($p < 0.001$). On Qwen3-14B FOLIO, where SC (86.27\%) far exceeds DeBERTa (49.51\%), the combo yields $\Delta \leq +0.49$\,pp (all $p = 1.0$). Aggregation gain shrinks with model capability: $+8.01$\,pp (Mistral-7B) to $+0.86$\,pp (Qwen3-14B thinking mode; Appendix~\ref{app:single-trace}). Across all conditions at $w{=}3$, combo gain tracks the capability gap (Figure~\ref{fig:capability-gap}): meaningful improvement emerges only when the verifier's standalone accuracy approaches or exceeds the generator's SC baseline."

<details><summary>↩ Tool result · `toolu_01UHsDFAQjr9H89q7vgFn2Lm`</summary>

```text
1	\section{Related Work}
2	\label{sec:related-work}
3	
4	\paragraph{Self-Consistency and extensions.}
5	SC selects the most frequent answer across chain-of-thought traces \citep{wang2023sc}. Extensions include universal SC for free-form generation \citep{chen2024usc}, confidence weighting \citep{taubenfeld2025cisc}, diverse prompting with step-aware verification \citep{li2023diverse}, program-based vote filtering \citep{toh2024notallvotes}, and reasoning-aware filtering \citep{wan2024rasc, kim2026reasc}. \citet{loo2025reevaluating} show that SC gains plateau early. Our Condorcet analysis explains this: $\kappa = 0.21$--$0.82$ compresses $K_{\text{eff}}$ to 1.20--3.62 for $K = 12$ (\S\ref{sec:effective-k}).
6	
7	\paragraph{Neuro-symbolic reasoning.}
8	Logic-LM \citep{pan2023logiclm} and LINC \citep{olausson2023linc} translate problems into formal logic for external provers; LINC documents ${\sim}$40\% translation errors. Recent extensions---CLOVER \citep{ryu2025clover}, DyLoS \citep{xu2026dylos}, Aristotle \citep{li2025aristotle}, FoVer \citep{wang2025fover}, VERGE \citep{singh2026verge}, LogicAgent \citep{luo2025logicagent}---add compositional translation, solver selection, decomposition, trained verifiers, and formal refinement, each introducing capabilities beyond the reasoning model. \citet{amonkar2025realitycheck} show that LLM formalization degrades with complexity; \citet{chan2026position} argue that logical soundness is not a reliable criterion for neurosymbolic verification with LLMs. Our oracle controls confirm the mechanism is sound ($+29.90$/$+14.22$\,pp FOLIO, $+11.50$\,pp ProofWriter); the failure localizes to self-extraction quality (\S\ref{sec:oracle}).
9	
10	\paragraph{Test-time compute and verification.}
11	Scaling test-time computation through additional traces \citep{snell2025scaling} faces the same correlation challenge: same-model attempts collapse into correlated solutions. Reasoning models (o1, DeepSeek-R1 \citep{guo2025deepseekr1}, QwQ \citep{qwen2024qwq}) extend thinking time but remain subject to the independence bottleneck: QwQ-32B produces $\kappa = 0.91$ and $C_0 = 2.6\%$, suggesting that longer reasoning chains intensify homogenization rather than alleviating it. Trained verifiers offer an alternative \citep{cobbe2021gsm8k, lightman2024prm, wang2024mathshepherd}; multi-agent debate refines answers through discussion \citep{liang2023encouraging, du2023improving}. Self-correction without external feedback degrades performance \citep{huang2024selfcorrect, stechly2025selfverification}, paralleling our confirmation bias finding (\S\ref{sec:confirmation-bias}).
12	
13	\paragraph{CJT and ensemble diversity.}
14	CJT \citep{condorcet1785} guarantees convergence under independent voting; \citet{ladha1992correlated} prove correlated voters reduce effective ensemble size; \citet{lefort2024condorcet_llm} apply CJT to LLM classification. We extend this by quantifying \emph{why} self-correction fails---same-model traces are correlated voters ($K_{\text{eff}}$ collapses)---and showing this persists even under formal verification (Z3-checked symbolic constraints) and at the process level (\S\ref{sec:process-kappa}).
15	
16	\paragraph{Positioning.}
17	Prior self-correction studies \citep{huang2024selfcorrect, stechly2025selfverification} establish qualitative negative results; we contribute reusable quantitative diagnostics that characterize the failure mode. Effective-$K$ (Eq.~\ref{eq:effk}) measures how much independent information $K$ traces actually carry. The bias ratio ($\text{BR}$; Eq.~\ref{eq:mirage}) quantifies the degree to which self-extracted constraints post-hoc rationalize the model's answer rather than independently verify it, connecting symbolic extraction to CoT faithfulness concerns \citep{turpin2024language}. Conclusion leakage rate ($C_D$) isolates answer-dependent constraint contamination at the step level. Together, these diagnostics show that formal Z3-checked verification inherits the same bias ($\text{BR} = 4.97\times$)---a finding invisible to prior work that examines only natural-language self-correction. The cross-architecture escape (\S\ref{sec:cross-arch}) yields a capability gap corollary: external verification helps only when the verifier's standalone accuracy exceeds the generator's SC baseline, providing a practical decision criterion absent from existing frameworks.
```

      "filePath": "/home/ubuntu/.agent-ml-research-idea_gen_0513_3/projects/symb_invariant_consensus/docs/paper/related_work.tex",
      "content": "\\section{Related Work}\n\\label{sec:related-work}\n\n\\paragraph{Self-Consistency and extensions.}\nSC selects the most frequent answer across chain-of-thought traces \\citep{wang2023sc}. Extensions include universal SC for free-form generation \\citep{chen2024usc}, confidence weighting \\citep{taubenfeld2025cisc}, diverse prompting with step-aware verification \\citep{li2023diverse}, program-based vote filtering \\citep{toh2024notallvotes}, and reasoning-aware filtering \\citep{wan2024rasc, kim2026reasc}. \\citet{loo2025reevaluating} show that SC gains plateau early. Our Condorcet analysis explains this: $\\kappa = 0.21$--$0.82$ compresses $K_{\\text{eff}}$ to 1.20--3.62 for $K = 12$ (\\S\\ref{sec:effective-k}).\n\n\\paragraph{Neuro-symbolic reasoning.}\nLogic-LM \\citep{pan2023logiclm} and LINC \\citep{olausson2023linc} translate problems into formal logic for external provers; LINC documents ${\\sim}$40\\% translation errors. Recent extensions---CLOVER \\citep{ryu2025clover}, DyLoS \\citep{xu2026dylos}, Aristotle \\citep{li2025aristotle}, FoVer \\citep{wang2025fover}, VERGE \\citep{singh2026verge}, LogicAgent \\citep{luo2025logicagent}---add compositional translation, solver selection, decomposition, trained verifiers, and formal refinement, each introducing capabilities beyond the reasoning model. \\citet{amonkar2025realitycheck} show that LLM formalization degrades with complexity; \\citet{chan2026position} argue that logical soundness is not a reliable criterion for neurosymbolic verification with LLMs. Our oracle controls confirm the mechanism is sound ($+29.90$/$+14.22$\\,pp FOLIO, $+11.50$\\,pp ProofWriter); the failure localizes to self-extraction quality (\\S\\ref{sec:oracle}).\n\n\\paragraph{Test-time compute and verification.}\nScaling test-time computation through additional traces \\citep{snell2025scaling} faces the same correlation challenge: same-model attempts collapse into correlated solutions. Reasoning models (o1, DeepSeek-R1 \\citep{guo2025deepseekr1}, QwQ \\citep{qwen2024qwq}) extend thinking time but remain subject to the independence bottleneck: QwQ-32B produces $\\kappa = 0.91$ and $C_0 = 2.6\\%$, suggesting that longer reasoning chains intensify homogenization rather than alleviating it. Trained verifiers offer an alternative \\citep{cobbe2021gsm8k, lightman2024prm, wang2024mathshepherd}; multi-agent debate refines answers through discussion \\citep{liang2023encouraging, du2023improving}. Self-correction without external feedback degrades performance \\citep{huang2024selfcorrect, stechly2025selfverification}, paralleling our confirmation bias finding (\\S\\ref{sec:confirmation-bias}).\n\n\\paragraph{CJT and ensemble diversity.}\nCJT \\citep{condorcet1785} guarantees convergence under independent voting; \\citet{ladha1992correlated} prove correlated voters reduce effective ensemble size; \\citet{lefort2024condorcet_llm} apply CJT to LLM classification. We extend this by quantifying \\emph{why} self-correction fails---same-model traces are correlated voters ($K_{\\text{eff}}$ collapses)---and showing this persists even under formal verification (Z3-checked symbolic constraints) and at the process level (\\S\\ref{sec:process-kappa}).\n\n\\paragraph{Positioning.}\nPrior self-correction studies \\citep{huang2024selfcorrect, stechly2025selfverification} establish qualitative negative results; we contribute reusable quantitative diagnostics that characterize the failure mode. Effective-$K$ (Eq.~\\ref{eq:effk}) measures how much independent information $K$ traces actually carry. The bias ratio ($\\text{BR}$; Eq.~\\ref{eq:mirage}) quantifies the degree to which self-extracted constraints post-hoc rationalize the model's answer rather than independently verify it, connecting symbolic extraction to CoT faithfulness concerns \\citep{turpin2024language}. Conclusion leakage rate ($C_D$) isolates answer-dependent constraint contamination at the step level. Together, these diagnostics show that formal Z3-checked verification inherits the same bias ($\\text{BR} = 4.97\\times$)---a finding invisible to prior work that examines only natural-language self-correction. The cross-architecture escape (\\S\\ref{sec:cross-arch}) yields a capability gap corollary: external verification helps only when the verifier's standalone accuracy exceeds the generator's SC baseline, providing a practical decision criterion absent from existing frameworks.",
      "numLines": 17,
      "startLine": 1,
      "totalLines": 17
    }
  },

```text
85	\subsection{The Symbolic Mirage Hypothesis}
86	\label{sec:mirage}
87	
88	We hypothesize that self-extracted symbolic structures are not independent verification signals but projections of the generating model's distributional biases. This \textbf{Symbolic Mirage} is specific to self-extracted constraints; symbolic methods employing independently sourced constraints remain effective (\S\ref{sec:oracle}). We quantify mirage intensity via the \emph{Bias Ratio}:
89	\begin{equation}
90	\label{eq:mirage}
91	\text{BR}(M,T) = \frac{P(c \text{ supports } a_M \mid a_M \neq a^*)}{P(c \text{ supports } a^* \mid a_M \neq a^*)},
92	\end{equation}
93	where $c$ is a self-extracted constraint, $a_M$ the model's answer, and $a^*$ the ground truth. $\text{BR} > 1$ indicates constraints project model bias rather than provide independent verification. The mirage degrades aggregation through two channels: correlated constraints compress $K_{\text{eff}} < 2$ (\emph{direct}; Eq.~\ref{eq:effk}), and answer-coupled constraints collapse MAX-SAT to vote counting (\emph{indirect}; Eq.~\ref{eq:degenerate}). Together with the oracle gap (\S\ref{sec:oracle}), BR bounds improvement: self-extracted constraints capture at most a $1/\text{BR}$ fraction of the oracle ceiling ($\Delta_{\max} \lesssim 6.0$\,pp for Mistral-7B, $\lesssim 3.6$\,pp for Qwen-14B; observed mean across conditions: $+2.12$\,pp). Three testable predictions follow: (1)~BR $> 1.0$ across architectures (\S\ref{sec:confirmation-bias}); (2)~no same-model intervention reduces BR below $1.0$ (\S\ref{sec:remediation}); (3)~only external verification escapes the mirage (\S\ref{sec:cross-arch}).
94	
95	\paragraph{Connection to CoT faithfulness.} BR operationalizes a specific form of unfaithful reasoning: the model's symbolic output reflects its answer rather than the problem's logical structure. \citet{turpin2024language} demonstrate that chain-of-thought explanations can be systematically unfaithful---models produce plausible reasoning that does not reflect their actual decision process. Our bias ratio extends this observation from natural-language explanations to formal symbolic outputs: even when constrained to produce Z3-compatible logical formulae, the model generates constraints that post-hoc rationalize its answer ($\text{BR} = 4.97\times$) rather than independently verify it. The formalization step thus provides a veneer of rigor without escaping the faithfulness problem.
96	
```

14	The 406M encoder-only DeBERTa and 7B decoder-only Mistral share no architecture, training data, or inference mechanism, satisfying the independence condition that same-model sampling cannot provide (\S\ref{sec:mirage}). This produces per-label complementarity: NLI excels on \textsc{False} problems (71.43\% vs.\ SC's 38.10\%) where the LLM is weakest, while SC dominates \textsc{Unknown} (68.12\% vs.\ 18.84\%). All three NLI architectures tested---DeBERTa, RoBERTa, and BART (all MNLI-trained)---produce significant improvements on ProofWriter at $w \geq 3$ (Appendix~\ref{app:multi-nli}), confirming the effect is not verifier-specific.
15	
16	\paragraph{Capability gap hypothesis.}
17	Cross-architecture verification succeeds when the verifier's standalone accuracy exceeds the generator's SC baseline. On ProofWriter, DeBERTa standalone (55.00\%) exceeds Mistral-7B SC (39.33\%), enabling $+10.67$\,pp at $w{=}5$ ($p < 0.001$). On Qwen3-14B FOLIO, where SC (86.27\%) far exceeds DeBERTa (49.51\%), the combo yields $\Delta \leq +0.49$\,pp (all $p = 1.0$). Aggregation gain shrinks with model capability: $+8.01$\,pp (Mistral-7B) to $+0.86$\,pp (Qwen3-14B thinking mode; Appendix~\ref{app:single-trace}). Across all conditions at $w{=}3$, combo gain tracks the capability gap (Figure~\ref{fig:capability-gap}): meaningful improvement emerges only when the verifier's standalone accuracy approaches or exceeds the generator's SC baseline.
18	
19	\begin{figure}[t]
20	\centering
21	\includegraphics[width=0.82\columnwidth]{figures/capability_gap_scatter}
22	\vspace{-3mm}
23	\caption{Combo gain vs.\ capability gap (verifier standalone $-$ generator SC) at $w{=}3$. Meaningful gain emerges when the gap is positive (verifier exceeds generator).}
24	\label{fig:capability-gap}
25	\vspace{-6mm}
26	\end{figure}
27	
28	\paragraph{Cross-generator results.}

Results hold across the full capability range: from Mistral-7B (54.41\% SC) to Qwen3-14B thinking mode (86.27\% SC), SICA delivers no significant gain. $\Delta$ = SICA $-$ SC (pp). $^b$In-context learning with gold logical examples. $\dagger$\,$n < 100$ (suggestive only). \textbf{Bold $\Delta$}: $p < 0.05$ (McNemar). {*}\,$p < 0.05$;\enspace{**}\,$p < 0.01$;\enspace{***}\,$p < 0.001$. Failure modes detailed in Appendix~\ref{app:failure-modes}.}

39	Mistral-7B & FOLIO (s456) & 204 & 55.88 & 55.88 & 0.00 & 1.000 \\
40	Mistral-7B & FOLIO (s789) & 204 & 54.41 & 53.92 & $-$0.49 & 1.000 \\

47	Qwen2.5-14B & FOLIO & 204 & 75.49 & 74.51 & $-$0.98 & 1.000 \\

65	
66	None of fifteen non-degenerate conditions shows a significant positive effect. Two show significant degradation: Mistral-7B on ProofWriter ($-2.83$\,pp, $p = 0.014$) and Qwen2.5-14B on ProofWriter ($-2.50$\,pp, $p = 0.011$). The intermediate-SC region (50--55\%) contains the only positive trends, none reaching $p < 0.05$. TOST equivalence tests \citep{lakens2017equivalence} ($\pm 2$\,pp) establish equivalence for 4 conditions; 5 show genuine differences (4 negative); 6 are underpowered (Appendix~\ref{app:tost}). We trace the failure to three mechanisms in \S\ref{sec:confirmation-bias}--\S\ref{sec:remediation}.

455	\includegraphics[width=0.85\textwidth]{figures/fig_app_interventions_summary.pdf}
456	\caption{Summary of 22+ intervention strategies grouped by category. All produce neutral-to-negative accuracy changes relative to Self-Consistency. The LLM-Debate bar is truncated at $-11.5$\,pp (actual: $-18.6$\,pp). The grey band indicates $\pm 1$\,pp noise zone.}
457	\label{fig:interventions-summary}
458	\end{figure*}
459	
460	
461	\section{Extraction Error Taxonomy}
462	\label{app:extraction}
463	\label{sec:extraction-taxonomy}
464	
465	Constraints must discriminate between candidate answers for MAX-SAT to produce a ranking different from majority vote. We measure discriminative power at two levels on Qwen2.5-14B across FOLIO and ProofWriter ($n = 804$). At the source-trace level, 39.2\% of total constraint weight originates from traces whose answer differs from the plurality, but only 12.1\% of individual constraints fall into this minority category. At the Z3 semantic level, 91.8\% of constraints [95\% CI: 91.2, 92.4] are technically discriminative, producing different satisfiability outcomes when evaluated against different candidate answers. Only 8.2\% are non-discriminative.
466	
467	The high discriminative rate does not translate into accuracy gains because discrimination mirrors the model's own answer distribution. Each trace produces constraints with its own variable names, so Z3 treats identically-intended propositions as unrelated symbols; among cross-answer constraint pairs, 56.4\% share zero Z3 variables. This variable isolation means constraints from different traces are trivially jointly satisfiable, and MAX-SAT retains nearly all of them regardless of source answer. Removing the 8.2\% non-discriminative constraints yields zero accuracy improvement. The discriminative majority does not provide independent signal; it reinforces the majority answer that SC would already select. This confirmation bias, not a lack of discrimination, explains why SICA degenerates to majority voting (Appendix~\ref{app:ablation}).
```

56	\end{equation}
57	where $\mathbf{v}_i^q$ is the TF-IDF vector of trace $t_i$ for question $q$. On Mistral-7B FOLIO ($n = 204$), mean ProcessSim is $0.69$, far exceeding answer-level $\kappa = 0.41$. The process--answer correlation is model-strength dependent: $r = -0.06$ ($p = 0.36$) on Mistral-7B, where answer diversity does not reflect reasoning diversity (Figure~\ref{fig:process-kappa}), rising to $r = +0.24$ ($p < 0.001$) on Qwen2.5-14B. This pattern generalizes across four families (Table~\ref{tab:cross-model-process}): the cosine--$\kappa$ gap narrows from $0.23$ (Mistral) to $0.02$ (Qwen-14B), meaning answer-level $\kappa$ already captures the bottleneck for strong models, while process-$\kappa$ reveals hidden homogeneity in weaker models where answer diversity masks process convergence. In all families, interventions targeting answer diversity (temperature scaling, diverse prompting) cannot alter the underlying reasoning paths.
58	
59	\begin{figure}[t]
60	\centering
61	\includegraphics[width=0.85\columnwidth]{figures/fig3_process_kappa_scatter.pdf}
62	\vspace{-2mm}
63	\caption{Answer agreement vs.\ CoT cosine similarity (Mistral-7B, FOLIO, $n{=}204$). Flat regression ($r = -0.06$, $p = 0.36$); stronger models show significant positive correlation ($r = +0.24$, Qwen-14B; Table~\ref{tab:cross-model-process}).}
64	\label{fig:process-kappa}
65	\end{figure}
66	
67	\subsection{Supporting Diagnostics}
68	\label{sec:diagnostics}
69	
70	Further diagnostics---constraint ablation, discriminativeness, entropy decomposition, and hyperparameter sensitivity---confirm the mechanism (Appendices~\ref{app:extraction}--\ref{app:entropy}).
71	
72	
73	\subsection{Remediation Analysis}
74	\label{sec:remediation}
75	\label{sec:interventions}
76	
77	Twenty-five-plus remediation strategies all produce neutral or negative results. At the prompt level, nine strategies fail: contrastive prompting ($\Delta = -1.1$\,pp), cross-model extraction ($\pm 1$\,pp), multi-temperature ($\Delta = 0.00$\,pp; Appendix Table~\ref{tab:remediation}). At the method level, ICG ($\Delta \approx 0$), Contrastive ($-9.31$\,pp, $p < 0.01$), and Debate ($-18.63$/$-23.00$\,pp, $p < 0.001$) all fail (Table~\ref{tab:method_interventions}). Fifteen further experiments produce neutral-to-negative results (Appendix Table~\ref{tab:additional_interventions}).
78	
79	\paragraph{Independence-accuracy trade-off.} SC, Diverse-Prompt, and ICG cluster at $\kappa \approx 0.40$--$0.47$; Debate reaches $\kappa = 0.15$ but collapses accuracy by $-18.6$\,pp. All families cluster above $\kappa = 0.40$, confirming a same-model ceiling: methods that increase independence destroy accuracy, and vice versa (Appendix Table~\ref{tab:kappa_generalization}).
```

More broadly, self-correction without external feedback degrades performance \\citep{huang2024selfcorrect, stechly2025selfverification}; our quantitative diagnostics (BR, Eff-$K$, process-$\\kappa$) provide reusable tools for predicting when any trace-aggregation method will fail.",
    "originalFile": "\\section{Related Work}\n\\label{sec:related-work}\n\n\\paragraph{Self-Consistency and extensions.}\nSC selects the most frequent answer across chain-of-thought traces \\citep{wang2023sc}. Extensions include universal SC for free-form generation \\citep{chen2024usc}, confidence weighting \\citep{taubenfeld2025cisc}, diverse prompting with step-aware verification \\citep{li2023diverse}, program-based vote filtering \\citep{toh2024notallvotes}, and reasoning-aware filtering \\citep{wan2024rasc, kim2026reasc}. \\citet{loo2025reevaluating} show that SC gains plateau early. Our Condorcet analysis explains this: $\\kappa = 0.21$--$0.82$ compresses $K_{\\text{eff}}$ to 1.20--3.62 for $K = 12$ (\\S\\ref{sec:effective-k}).\n\n\\paragraph{Neuro-symbolic reasoning.}\nLogic-LM \\citep{pan2023logiclm} and LINC \\citep{olausson2023linc} translate problems into formal logic for external provers; LINC documents ${\\sim}$40\\% translation errors. Recent extensions---CLOVER \\citep{ryu2025clover}, DyLoS \\citep{xu2026dylos}, Aristotle \\citep{li2025aristotle}, FoVer \\citep{wang2025fover}, VERGE \\citep{singh2026verge}, LogicAgent \\citep{luo2025logicagent}---add compositional translation, solver selection, decomposition, trained verifiers, and formal refinement, each introducing capabilities beyond the reasoning model. \\citet{amonkar2025realitycheck} show that LLM formalization degrades with complexity; \\citet{chan2026position} argue that logical soundness is not a reliable criterion for neurosymbolic verification with LLMs. Our oracle controls confirm the mechanism is sound ($+29.90$/$+14.22$\\,pp FOLIO, $+11.50$\\,pp ProofWriter); the failure localizes to self-extraction quality (\\S\\ref{sec:oracle}).\n\n\\paragraph{Test-time compute and verification.}\nScaling test-time computation through additional traces \\citep{snell2025scaling} faces the same correlation challenge: same-model attempts collapse into correlated solutions. Reasoning models (o1, DeepSeek-R1 \\citep{guo2025deepseekr1}, QwQ \\citep{qwen2024qwq}) extend thinking time but remain subject to the independence bottleneck: QwQ-32B produces $\\kappa = 0.91$ and $C_0 = 2.6\\%$, suggesting that longer reasoning chains intensify homogenization rather than alleviating it. Trained verifiers offer an alternative \\citep{cobbe2021gsm8k, lightman2024prm, wang2024mathshepherd}; multi-agent debate refines answers through discussion \\citep{liang2023encouraging, du2023improving}. Self-correction without external feedback degrades performance \\citep{huang2024selfcorrect, stechly2025selfverification}, paralleling our confirmation bias finding (\\S\\ref{sec:confirmation-bias}).\n\n\\paragraph{CJT and ensemble diversity.}\nCJT \\citep{condorcet1785} guarantees convergence under independent voting; \\citet{ladha1992correlated} prove correlated voters reduce effective ensemble size; \\citet{lefort2024condorcet_llm} apply CJT to LLM classification. Effective-$K$ (Eq.~\\ref{eq:effk}) measures how much independent information $K$ traces actually carry. The bias ratio ($\\text{BR}$; Eq.~\\ref{eq:mirage}) quantifies the degree to which self-extracted constraints post-hoc rationalize the model's answer rather than independently verify it, connecting symbolic extraction to CoT faithfulness concerns \\citep{turpin2024language}. Conclusion leakage rate ($C_D$) isolates answer-dependent constraint contamination at the step level. Together, these diagnostics show that formal Z3-checked verification inherits the same bias ($\\text{BR} = 4.97\\times$)---a finding invisible to prior work that examines only natural-language self-correction. The cross-architecture escape (\\S\\ref{sec:cross-arch}) yields a capability gap corollary: external verification helps only when the verifier's standalone accuracy exceeds the generator's SC baseline, providing a practical decision criterion absent from existing frameworks.",

{

  "parentUuid": "0968dd8b-3b8e-410a-896c-2d36b886506c",
  "isSidechain": false,
  "promptId": "37b37ff4-1dfc-4e5f-a848-79e58dfc1bca",

  "toolUseResult": {
    "filePath": "/home/ubuntu/.agent-ml-research-idea_gen_0513_3/projects/symb_invariant_consensus/docs/paper/method.tex",
    "oldString": "Three testable predictions follow: (1)~BR $> 1.0$ across architectures (\\S\\ref{sec:confirmation-bias}); (2)~no same-model intervention reduces BR below $1.0$ (\\S\\ref{sec:remediation}); (3)~only external verification escapes the mirage (\\S\\ref{sec:cross-arch}).",
    "newString": "If chain-of-thought traces are unfaithful representations of the model's actual reasoning process \\citep{turpin2024language}, self-extracted constraints inherit representation bias rather than reasoning content---making the mirage an inherent property of extraction from opaque models, not merely a quality issue. Three testable predictions follow: (1)~BR $> 1.0$ across architectures (\\S\\ref{sec:confirmation-bias}); (2)~no same-model intervention reduces BR below $1.0$ (\\S\\ref{sec:remediation}); (3)~only external verification escapes the mirage (\\S\\ref{sec:cross-arch}).",
    "originalFile": "\\section{Background \\& Setup}\n\\label{sec:method}\n\n\\subsection{Self-Consistency and the Condorcet Independence Assumption}\n\\label{sec:condorcet}\n\nSelf-Consistency (SC) \\citep{wang2023sc} samples $K$ reasoning traces and selects the majority answer. The Condorcet Jury Theorem (CJT) \\citep{condorcet1785} guarantees that if $K$ independent voters are each correct with probability $p > 0.5$, the majority converges to truth as $K \\to \\infty$ \\citep{austensmith1996}. LLM traces violate this independence: all $K$ samples come from the same model with only temperature as variation. Following \\citet{ladha1992correlated} and the design-effect framework of \\citet{kish1965survey}, we define effective sample size using Fleiss' $\\kappa$ \\citep{fleiss1971kappa} as proxy for intraclass correlation:\n\\begin{equation}\n\\label{eq:effk}\nK_{\\text{eff}} = \\frac{K}{1 + (K - 1) \\cdot \\kappa}.\n\\end{equation}\nIn our experiments (\\S\\ref{sec:effective-k}), $\\kappa$ ranges from 0.21 to 0.82 across instruction-tuned pairs (0.91 for thinking mode), yielding $K_{\\text{eff}} \\approx 1.2\\text{--}3.6$ for $K = 12$. Note that $\\kappa$ is a qualitative proxy for the intraclass correlation coefficient (ICC); we use $K_{\\text{eff}}$ as an interpretive indicator rather than a precise statistical estimator. This \\emph{independence bottleneck} motivates our investigation.\n\n\\subsection{SICA Pipeline}\n\\label{sec:pipeline}\n\nSymbolic Invariant Consensus Aggregation (SICA) extends SC by extracting formal constraints from traces and selecting the answer best supported by the satisfiable subset (Figure~\\ref{fig:pipeline}).\n\n\\paragraph{Trace generation.} Given question $q$, an LLM generates $K$ traces $\\{t_1, \\ldots, t_K\\}$ at temperature $T = 0.7$. Each trace $t_i$ yields answer $a_i$.\n\n\\paragraph{Constraint extraction.}\\label{sec:constraint-extraction} The same LLM extracts FOL constraints from each trace at $T_{\\text{ext}} = 0.3$. The extraction prompt requests JSON output with constraint type, expression, and Z3-compatible formula (Appendix~\\ref{app:prompts}). The \\emph{extraction rate} is the fraction of traces yielding at least one parseable constraint.\n\n\\paragraph{Deduplication and MAX-SAT.} Constraints are deduplicated by syntactic equivalence; each unique constraint $c_m$ receives weight $w_m$ equal to the number of source traces. The objective finds the largest-weight satisfiable subset:\n\\begin{equation}\n\\label{eq:maxsat}\n\\mathcal{C}^* = \\arg\\max_{\\mathcal{S} \\subseteq \\mathcal{C}} \\sum_{c \\in \\mathcal{S}} w_c \\quad \\text{s.t.} \\quad \\mathcal{S} \\text{ is satisfiable.}\n\\end{equation}\nZ3 \\citep{demoura2008z3} solves this with a 10-second timeout. Answer assertion constraints ($\\texttt{answer\\_a} == \\texttt{True}$, weight 1) ensure every candidate receives a nonzero base score, making SICA a strict generalization of SC.\n\n\\paragraph{Scoring.} Let $\\mathcal{C}^{\\setminus} = \\mathcal{C} \\setminus \\mathcal{C}^*$. Each candidate $a$ is scored by:\n\\begin{equation}\n\\label{eq:score}\n\\text{score}(a) = \\sum_{c \\in \\mathcal{C}^*} w_c \\cdot \\sigma(c, a) - \\alpha \\sum_{c \\in \\mathcal{C}^{\\setminus}} w_c \\cdot \\sigma(c, a),\n\\end{equation}\nwhere $\\sigma(c, a) = 1$ if $c$ was extracted from any trace producing answer $a$, and $\\alpha = 0.5$. The selected answer is $a^* = \\arg\\max_{a} \\text{score}(a)$.\n\n\\paragraph{Degeneration condition.} If self-extracted constraints are coupled to their source answer, MAX-SAT retains nearly all ($|\\mathcal{C}^{\\setminus}| \\approx 0$), and scoring degenerates to:\n\\begin{equation}\n\\label{eq:degenerate}\n\\text{score}(a) \\approx \\sum_{\\substack{c \\in \\mathcal{C}^* \\\\ \\text{source}(c) \\to a}} w_c,\n\\end{equation}\nequivalent to majority voting. We show in \\S\\ref{sec:confirmation-bias} that this degeneration occurs in practice.\n\n\\paragraph{The $C_0$ diagnostic.} The multi-candidate rate $C_0 = |\\{q : |\\mathcal{A}(q)| > 1\\}| / |\\mathcal{Q}|$ is a zero-cost diagnostic computable before extraction. When $C_0 < 5\\%$, SICA produces the same output as SC. The penalty weight $\\alpha = 0.5$ is not a critical hyperparameter: accuracy varies by at most $0.49$\\,pp across $\\alpha \\in \\{0.0, 0.1, 0.3, 0.5, 0.7, 1.0\\}$ on Mistral-7B FOLIO.\n\n\\begin{figure}[t]\n\\centering\n\\small\n\\begin{tikzpicture}[\n    node distance=0.4cm and 0.35cm,\n    box/.style={draw, rounded corners=2pt, minimum height=0.6cm,\n                text width=1.6cm, align=center, font=\\scriptsize},\n    arr/.style={-{Stealth[length=4pt]}, thick},\n    darr/.style={-{Stealth[length=4pt]}, thick, densely dashed},\n    lbl/.style={font=\\scriptsize, text=gray!70!black},\n    ibottle/.style={draw=red!70!black, densely dashed, rounded corners=3pt,\n                    inner sep=3pt},\n]\n\\node[box] (q) {Question $q$};\n\\node[box, right=of q] (sample) {Sample $K$\\\\traces};\n\\node[box, right=of sample] (extract) {Extract FOL\\\\constraints};\n\\node[box, right=of extract] (dedup) {Dedup.\\ +\\\\weight};\n\\node[box, right=of dedup] (maxsat) {MAX-SAT\\\\(Z3)};\n\n\\node[box, below=0.7cm of maxsat, fill=gray!12] (ans) {Answer $a^*$};\n\\node[box, below=0.7cm of sample, fill=gray!12] (sc) {Majority\\\\vote};\n\n\\draw[arr] (q) -- (sample);\n\\draw[arr] (sample) -- (extract);\n\\draw[arr] (extract) -- (dedup);\n\\draw[arr] (dedup) -- (maxsat);\n\\draw[arr] (maxsat) -- (ans);\n\\draw[arr] (sample) -- node[lbl, right, yshift=0pt] {SC} (sc);\n\n\\node[lbl, above=0.01cm of maxsat] {SICA};\n\n\\node[ibottle, fit=(sample)(extract), label={[font=\\scriptsize\\itshape, text=red!70!black]above:Independence Bottleneck}] {};\n\n\\draw[darr, gray!60] ([xshift=-0.1cm]dedup.south) -- ++(0,-0.35) node[lbl, below, yshift=-0.05cm] {oracle: gold FOL};\n\\end{tikzpicture}\n\\caption{The SICA pipeline. SC selects the majority answer from $K$ traces. SICA extracts FOL constraints, deduplicates, and solves MAX-SAT via Z3. Red dashed box: the independence bottleneck. Dashed arrow: oracle variant with gold FOL.}\n\\label{fig:pipeline}\n\\end{figure}\n\n\\subsection{The Symbolic Mirage Hypothesis}\n\\label{sec:mirage}\n\nWe hypothesize that self-extracted symbolic structures are not independent verification signals but projections of the generating model's distributional biases. This \\textbf{Symbolic Mirage} is specific to self-extracted constraints; symbolic methods employing independently sourced constraints remain effective (\\S\\ref{sec:oracle}). We quantify mirage intensity via the \\emph{Bias Ratio}:\n\\begin{equation}\n\\label{eq:mirage}\n\\text{BR}(M,T) = \\frac{P(c \\text{ supports } a_M \\mid a_M \\neq a^*)}{P(c \\text{ supports } a^* \\mid a_M \\neq a^*)},\n\\end{equation}\nwhere $c$ is a self-extracted constraint, $a_M$ the model's answer, and $a^*$ the ground truth. $\\text{BR} > 1$ indicates constraints project model bias rather than provide independent verification. The mirage degrades aggregation through two channels: correlated constraints compress $K_{\\text{eff}} < 2$ (\\emph{direct}; Eq.~\\ref{eq:effk}), and answer-coupled constraints collapse MAX-SAT to vote counting (\\emph{indirect}; Eq.~\\ref{eq:degenerate}). Together with the oracle gap (\\S\\ref{sec:oracle}), BR bounds improvement: self-extracted constraints capture at most a $1/\\text{BR}$ fraction of the oracle ceiling ($\\Delta_{\\max} \\lesssim 6.0$\\,pp for Mistral-7B, $\\lesssim 3.6$\\,pp for Qwen-14B; observed mean across conditions: $+2.12$\\,pp). Three testable predictions follow: (1)~BR $> 1.0$ across architectures (\\S\\ref{sec:confirmation-bias}); (2)~no same-model intervention reduces BR below $1.0$ (\\S\\ref{sec:remediation}); (3)~only external verification escapes the mirage (\\S\\ref{sec:cross-arch}).\n\n\\paragraph{Connection to CoT faithfulness.} BR operationalizes a specific form of unfaithful reasoning: the model's symbolic output reflects its answer rather than the problem's logical structure. \\citet{turpin2024language} demonstrate that chain-of-thought explanations can be systematically unfaithful---models produce plausible reasoning that does not reflect their actual decision process. Our bias ratio extends this observation from natural-language explanations to formal symbolic outputs: even when constrained to produce Z3-compatible logical formulae, the model generates constraints that post-hoc rationalize its answer ($\\text{BR} = 4.97\\times$) rather than independently verify it. The formalization step thus provides a veneer of rigor without escaping the faithfulness problem.\n",

    "originalFile": "\\section{Cross-Architecture Proof-of-Concept}\n\\label{sec:solutions}\n\\label{sec:cross-arch}\n\nThe analyses in \\S\\ref{sec:confirmation-bias}--\\S\\ref{sec:remediation} confirm all three predictions of the Symbolic Mirage hypothesis (\\S\\ref{sec:mirage}): self-extracted constraints inherit the generating model's bias. The oracle gap (\\S\\ref{sec:oracle}) confirms the mechanism is sound given uncontaminated constraints; the corrective signal must come from outside the model's distribution.\n\n\\paragraph{Method.}\nWe test this with DeBERTa-large \\citep{he2021deberta} (406M, encoder-only), fine-tuned on MultiNLI \\citep{williams2018mnli}, classifying (premise, conclusion) pairs into \\textsc{entailment}, \\textsc{contradiction}, or \\textsc{neutral}. In combo mode, the NLI verdict is integrated with SC's $K$ votes via weight $w$: each NLI-supporting vote counts $w$ times. We evaluate on Mistral-7B FOLIO ($n = 204$) and ProofWriter depth-5 ($n = 600$), because NLI classification requires (premise, conclusion) pairs that these datasets naturally provide; multiple-choice datasets (LogiQA) lack this structure.\n\n\\paragraph{Main result.}\nDeBERTa NLI alone achieves 49.51\\% on FOLIO (below SC's 55.88\\%) and 55.00\\% on ProofWriter (above SC's 39.33\\%). On FOLIO ($n = 204$), combo mode yields $+1.96$\\,pp at $w{=}1$ and $+2.94$\\,pp at $w{=}3$ (both n.s.). On ProofWriter ($n = 600$), $w{=}3$ achieves $+3.50$\\,pp ($p = 0.013$) and $w{=}5$ reaches $+10.67$\\,pp ($p < 0.001$)---while same-model SICA \\emph{degrades} ProofWriter ($-2.83$\\,pp, $p = 0.014$; Table~\\ref{tab:main_results}), illustrating that corrective signal must originate outside the model's distribution (\\S\\ref{sec:mirage}). We report $w{=}3$ as primary; higher weights risk overfitting. After Benjamini--Hochberg correction ($m{=}6$), ProofWriter $w{=}3$ remains significant (adjusted $p = 0.038$) and $w{=}5$ ($p < 0.001$); FOLIO comparisons are non-significant. Per-label caveat: DeBERTa over-predicts \\textsc{False} on ProofWriter (67.8\\%) and performs poorly on \\textsc{Unknown} (3.5\\% accuracy), as NLI's ``neutral'' fails to capture logical unknowability.\n\n\\paragraph{Why it works.}\nThe 406M encoder-only DeBERTa and 7B decoder-only Mistral share no architecture, training data, or inference mechanism, satisfying the independence condition that same-model sampling cannot provide (\\S\\ref{sec:mirage}). This produces per-label complementarity: NLI excels on \\textsc{False} problems (71.43\\% vs.\\ SC's 38.10\\%) where the LLM is weakest, while SC dominates \\textsc{Unknown} (68.12\\% vs.\\ 18.84\\%). On ProofWriter, DeBERTa standalone (55.00\\%) exceeds Mistral-7B SC (39.33\\%), enabling $+10.67$\\,pp at $w{=}5$ ($p < 0.001$). On Qwen3-14B FOLIO, where SC (86.27\\%) far exceeds DeBERTa (49.51\\%), the combo yields $\\Delta \\leq +0.49$\\,pp (all $p = 1.0$). Aggregation gain shrinks with model capability: $+8.01$\\,pp (Mistral-7B) to $+0.86$\\,pp (Qwen3-14B thinking mode; Appendix~\\ref{app:single-trace}). Across all conditions at $w{=}3$, combo gain tracks the capability gap (Figure~\\ref{fig:capability-gap}): meaningful improvement emerges only when the verifier's standalone accuracy approaches or exceeds the generator's SC baseline.\n\n\\begin{figure}[t]\n\\centering\n\\includegraphics[width=0.82\\columnwidth]{figures/capability_gap_scatter}\n\\vspace{-3mm}\n\\caption{Combo gain vs.\\ capability gap (verifier standalone $-$ generator SC) at $w{=}3$. Meaningful gain emerges when the gap is positive (verifier exceeds generator).}\n\\label{fig:capability-gap}\n\\vspace{-6mm}\n\\end{figure}\n\n\\paragraph{Cross-generator results.}\nTable~\\ref{tab:cross-generator-nli} extends the NLI combo across two generators and four verifiers. Three of four Mistral-7B verifiers produce significant gains ($p < 0.001$), while neither Qwen3-14B condition reaches significance. The pattern is consistent: NLI escape is strongest when the generator is weak (SC $= 39.33\\%$) and the verifier's standalone accuracy approaches or exceeds the generator's baseline. When the generator is strong (SC $= 85.00\\%$), even architecturally independent verifiers cannot contribute corrective signal, confirming that the capability gap is the binding constraint rather than architectural diversity alone.\n\n\\begin{table}[t]\n\\centering\n\\small\n\\caption{Cross-generator NLI combo on ProofWriter depth-5 ($n{=}600$, $w{=}5$). Beyond accuracy comparisons, we conduct diagnostic analyses of symbolic confirmation bias and process-level trace independence (\\S\\ref{sec:analysis}).\n\n\\paragraph{Models.} Qwen2.5-14B-Instruct, Qwen2.5-7B-Instruct, Qwen3-14B (non-thinking mode)~\\citep{yang2024qwen25}, Mistral-7B-Instruct-v0.3~\\citep{jiang2023mistral}, LLaMA-3.1-8B-Instruct~\\citep{dubey2024llama3}, and QwQ-32B~\\citep{qwen2024qwq} (7B--32B parameters).\n\n\\paragraph{Datasets.} FOLIO~\\citep{han2022folio} ($n{=}204$, with gold logical annotations), ProofWriter depth-5~\\citep{tafjord2021proofwriter} ($n{=}600$), LogiQA~\\citep{liu2020logiqa} ($n{=}200$). I found the paper for you! Here are the details:

**Paper Title:** Large Language Models are Better Reasoners with Self-Verification

**Authors:** Yixuan Weng, Minjun Zhu, Fei Xia, Bin Li, Shizhu He, Shengping Liu, Bin Sun, Kang Liu, and Jun Zhao

**Publication:** Findings of the Association for Computational Linguistics: EMNLP 2023, pages 2550–2575, Singapore

**Key Concept:** By performing a backward verification of the answers that LLM deduced for itself, we can obtain interpretable answer validation scores to select the candidate answer with the highest score.

**Results:** Experimental results demonstrate that the proposed method can improve the reasoning performance on various arithmetic, commonsense, and logical reasoning datasets.

**Note:** This paper was published at EMNLP 2023, not NeurIPS. The code is publicly available at: https://github.com/WENGSYX/Self-Verification

I found the paper! Here's a summary of the search results:

## Self-Refine: Iterative Refinement with Self-Feedback

The paper was published at NeurIPS 2023 and introduces Self-Refine, an approach for improving initial outputs from LLMs through iterative feedback and refinement, motivated by how humans refine their written text.

**Key Concept:**
The main idea is to generate an initial output using an LLM; then, the same LLM provides feedback for its output and uses it to refine itself, iteratively.

**Authors:**
Aman Madaan, Niket Tandon, Prakhar Gupta, Skyler Hallinan, Luyu Gao, Sarah Wiegreffe, Uri Alon, Nouha Dziri, Shrimai Prabhumoye, Yiming Yang, Bodhisattwa Prasad Majumder, Shashank Gupta, Katherine Hermann, Sean Welleck, Amir Yazdanbakhsh, and Peter Clark

**Advantages:**
Self-Refine does not require any supervised training data, additional training, or reinforcement learning, and instead uses a single LLM as the generator, refiner, and critic.

**Evaluation:**
The approach was evaluated across 7 diverse tasks, ranging from dialog response generation to mathematical reasoning, using state-of-the-art LLMs (GPT-3.5, ChatGPT, and GPT-4), with outputs generated with Self-Refine being preferred by both humans and automatic metrics.

The paper is available on arXiv (2303.17651), GitHub, and the official NeurIPS proceedings.


REMINDER: You MUST include the sources above in your response to the user using markdown hyperlinks.

Here's a summary of the search results:\n\n## Self-Refine: Iterative Refinement with Self-Feedback\n\nThe paper was published at NeurIPS 2023 and introduces Self-Refine, an approach for improving initial outputs from LLMs through iterative feedback and refinement, motivated by how humans refine their written text.\n\n**Key Concept:**\nThe main idea is to generate an initial output using an LLM; then, the same LLM provides feedback for its output and uses it to refine itself, iteratively.\n\n**Authors:**\nAman Madaan, Niket Tandon, Prakhar Gupta, Skyler Hallinan, Luyu Gao, Sarah Wiegreffe, Uri Alon, Nouha Dziri, Shrimai Prabhumoye, Yiming Yang, Bodhisattwa Prasad Majumder, Shashank Gupta, Katherine Hermann, Sean Welleck, Amir Yazdanbakhsh, and Peter Clark\n\n**Advantages:**\nSelf-Refine does not require any supervised training data, additional training, or reinforcement learning, and instead uses a single LLM as the generator, refiner, and critic.\n\n**Evaluation:**\nThe approach was evaluated across 7 diverse tasks, ranging from dialog response generation to mathematical reasoning, using state-of-the-art LLMs (GPT-3.5, ChatGPT, and GPT-4), with outputs generated with Self-Refine being preferred by both humans and automatic metrics.\n\nThe paper is available on arXiv (2303.17651), GitHub, and the official NeurIPS proceedings."
    ],
    "durationSeconds": 9.514145929000005

On Mistral-7B FOLIO ($n = 204$), mean ProcessSim is $0.69$, far exceeding answer-level $\kappa = 0.41$. These measures are uncorrelated ($r = -0.06$, $p = 0.36$): problems with diverse answers do not exhibit diverse reasoning (Figure~\ref{fig:process-kappa}). This gap implies that interventions targeting answer diversity (temperature scaling, diverse prompting) may spread answers without altering reasoning paths.

Answer-level self-verification \\citep{weng2023selfverification} and iterative self-refinement \\citep{madaan2023selfrefine} report gains using natural-language feedback, but neither employs formal symbolic verification.",
    "originalFile": "\\section{Related Work}\n\\label{sec:related-work}\n\n\\paragraph{Self-Consistency and extensions.}\nSC selects the most frequent answer across chain-of-thought traces \\citep{wang2023sc}. Extensions include universal SC for free-form generation \\citep{chen2024usc}, confidence weighting \\citep{taubenfeld2025cisc}, diverse prompting with step-aware verification \\citep{li2023diverse}, program-based vote filtering \\citep{toh2024notallvotes}, and reasoning-aware filtering \\citep{wan2024rasc, kim2026reasc}. \\citet{loo2025reevaluating} show that SC gains plateau early. Our Condorcet analysis explains this: $\\kappa = 0.21$--$0.82$ compresses $K_{\\text{eff}}$ to 1.20--3.62 for $K = 12$ (\\S\\ref{sec:effective-k}).\n\n\\paragraph{Neuro-symbolic reasoning.}\nLogic-LM \\citep{pan2023logiclm} and LINC \\citep{olausson2023linc} translate problems into formal logic for external provers; LINC documents ${\\sim}$40\\% translation errors. Recent extensions---CLOVER \\citep{ryu2025clover}, DyLoS \\citep{xu2026dylos}, Aristotle \\citep{li2025aristotle}, FoVer \\citep{wang2025fover}, VERGE \\citep{singh2026verge}, LogicAgent \\citep{luo2025logicagent}---add compositional translation, solver selection, decomposition, trained verifiers, and formal refinement, each introducing capabilities beyond the reasoning model. \\citet{amonkar2025realitycheck} show that LLM formalization degrades with complexity; \\citet{chan2026position} argue that logical soundness is not a reliable criterion for neurosymbolic verification with LLMs. Our oracle controls confirm the mechanism is sound ($+29.90$/$+14.22$\\,pp FOLIO, $+11.50$\\,pp ProofWriter); the failure localizes to self-extraction quality (\\S\\ref{sec:oracle}).\n\n\\paragraph{Test-time compute and verification.}\nScaling test-time computation through additional traces \\citep{snell2025scaling} faces the same correlation challenge: same-model attempts collapse into correlated solutions. Reasoning models (o1, DeepSeek-R1 \\citep{guo2025deepseekr1}, QwQ \\citep{qwen2024qwq}) extend thinking time but remain subject to the independence bottleneck: QwQ-32B produces $\\kappa = 0.91$ and $C_0 = 2.6\\%$, suggesting that longer reasoning chains intensify homogenization rather than alleviating it. Trained verifiers offer an alternative \\citep{cobbe2021gsm8k, lightman2024prm, wang2024mathshepherd}; multi-agent debate refines answers through discussion \\citep{liang2023encouraging, du2023improving}. A natural hypothesis is that deterministic formal verification---where a Z3 solver provides logically guaranteed judgments---might bypass natural-language self-correction limitations by introducing a non-neural reasoning channel. We test this systematically and find the bottleneck shifts from the verifier to the extractor: self-extracted constraints inherit the model's bias regardless of verification formalism (\\S\\ref{sec:confirmation-bias}). More broadly, self-correction without external feedback degrades performance \\citep{huang2024selfcorrect, stechly2025selfverification}; our quantitative diagnostics (BR, Eff-$K$, process-$\\kappa$) provide reusable tools for predicting when any trace-aggregation method will fail.\n\n\\paragraph{CJT and ensemble diversity.}\nCJT \\citep{condorcet1785} guarantees convergence under independent voting; \\citet{ladha1992correlated} prove correlated voters reduce effective ensemble size; \\citet{lefort2024condorcet_llm} apply CJT to LLM classification. We extend this by quantifying \\emph{why} self-correction fails---same-model traces are correlated voters ($K_{\\text{eff}}$ collapses)---and showing this persists even under formal verification (Z3-checked symbolic constraints) and at the process level (\\S\\ref{sec:process-kappa}).\n\n\\paragraph{Positioning.}\nPrior self-correction studies \\citep{huang2024selfcorrect, stechly2025selfverification} establish qualitative negative results; we contribute reusable quantitative diagnostics that characterize the failure mode. Effective-$K$ (Eq.~\\ref{eq:effk}) measures how much independent information $K$ traces actually carry. The bias ratio ($\\text{BR}$; Eq.~\\ref{eq:mirage}) quantifies the degree to which self-extracted constraints post-hoc rationalize the model's answer rather than independently verify it, connecting symbolic extraction to CoT faithfulness concerns \\citep{turpin2024language}. Conclusion leakage rate ($C_D$) isolates answer-dependent constraint contamination at the step level. Together, these diagnostics show that formal Z3-checked verification inherits the same bias ($\\text{BR} = 4.97\\times$)---a finding invisible to prior work that examines only natural-language self-correction. The cross-architecture escape (\\S\\ref{sec:cross-arch}) yields a capability gap corollary: external verification helps only when the verifier's standalone accuracy exceeds the generator's SC baseline, providing a practical decision criterion absent from existing frameworks.",

    "oldString": "\\begin{figure}[t]\n\\centering\n\\includegraphics[width=0.85\\columnwidth]{figures/fig3_process_kappa_scatter.pdf}\n\\vspace{-2mm}\n\\caption{Answer agreement vs.\\ CoT cosine similarity (Mistral-7B, FOLIO, $n{=}204$). Flat regression ($r = -0.06$, $p = 0.36$); stronger models show significant positive correlation ($r = +0.24$, Qwen-14B; Table~\\ref{tab:cross-model-process}).}\n\\label{fig:process-kappa}\n\\end{figure}",
    "newString": "",
    "originalFile": "\\section{Analysis}\n\\label{sec:analysis}\n\nThis section tests the three predictions of the Symbolic Mirage hypothesis (\\S\\ref{sec:mirage}): trace correlation and $K_{\\text{eff}}$ collapse (\\S\\ref{sec:effective-k}), systematic confirmation bias with $\\text{BR} > 1$ across architectures (\\S\\ref{sec:confirmation-bias}), process-level independence (\\S\\ref{sec:process-kappa}), supporting diagnostics (\\S\\ref{sec:diagnostics}), and the inescapability of the mirage under twenty-plus same-model interventions (\\S\\ref{sec:remediation}).\n\n\\subsection{Condorcet Effective-K}\n\\label{sec:effective-k}\n\nTable~\\ref{tab:independence} reports Fleiss' $\\kappa$ and Effective-K (Eq.~\\ref{eq:effk}) across 15 conditions at $K = 12$. Among instruction-tuned models, $\\kappa$ ranges from 0.21 to 0.82, yielding $K_{\\text{eff}} = 1.20\\text{--}3.62$. The four Mistral-7B FOLIO seeds produce $\\kappa \\in [0.41, 0.51]$, yet $\\Delta$ ranges from $+3.43$ to $-0.49$\\,pp---seed-level constraint quality, not independence alone, appears to influence the variation. Two conditions are jointly necessary for positive $\\Delta$: sufficient $K_{\\text{eff}}$ ($\\leq 1.30$ never improves) and sufficient SC accuracy ($K_{\\text{eff}} = 3.62$ with SC $= 39\\%$ produces significant degradation). Cross-model validation confirms universality: all four families produce $\\kappa \\geq 0.41$ (Table~\\ref{tab:kappa_generalization}; Appendix~\\ref{app:cross-model-kappa}).\n\n\\begin{table}[t]\n\\centering\n\\small\n\\caption{Trace independence metrics ($K = 12$, sorted by $\\kappa$ descending). $\\kappa$: Fleiss' Kappa. $K_{\\text{eff}}$: Effective-K (Eq.~\\ref{eq:effk}). SC: Self-Consistency baseline. $\\Delta$: SICA $-$ SC (pp). {*}\\,$p < 0.05$; positive $\\Delta$ values do not reach significance. multi-T $= T \\in \\{0.3, 0.7, 1.2\\}$, 4 traces each.}\n\\label{tab:independence}\n\\begin{tabular}{@{}llccrr@{}}\n\\toprule\nModel & Condition & $\\kappa$ & $K_{\\text{eff}}$ & SC (\\%) & $\\Delta$ (pp) \\\\\n\\midrule\nQwen3 (think) & FOLIO & 0.91 & 1.09 & ${\\sim}$84 & ${\\sim}$0 \\\\\nQwen2.5-14B & LogiQA & 0.82 & 1.20 & 78.50 & $-$0.50 \\\\\nQwen3 & FOLIO & 0.77 & 1.26 & 84.80 & $-$0.49 \\\\\nQwen2.5-14B & FOLIO & 0.74 & 1.30 & 75.49 & $-$0.98 \\\\\nQwen2.5-7B & FOLIO & 0.66 & 1.46 & 75.98 & $+$0.49 \\\\\n\\addlinespace\nMistral-7B ($T{=}0.3$) & FOLIO & 0.53 & 1.77 & 58.33 & $-$1.47 \\\\\nMistral-7B & LogiQA & 0.51 & 1.81 & 51.00 & $+$0.50 \\\\\nMistral-7B (s456) & FOLIO & 0.51 & 1.82 & 55.88 & 0.00 \\\\\nMistral-7B & FOLIO & 0.50 & 1.84 & 55.88 & $+$3.43 \\\\\nMistral-7B (s123) & FOLIO & 0.49 & 1.88 & 54.41 & $+$2.94 \\\\\nMistral-7B (s789) & FOLIO & 0.41 & 2.17 & 54.41 & $-$0.49 \\\\\nMistral-7B ($T{=}0.5$) & FOLIO & 0.52 & 1.80 & 58.33 & $-$0.98 \\\\\nMistral-7B (multi-T) & FOLIO & 0.48 & 1.91 & 57.84 & 0.00 \\\\\nMistral-7B ($T{=}1.0$) & FOLIO & 0.48 & 1.91 & 55.39 & 0.00 \\\\\nMistral-7B & PW-D5 & 0.21 & 3.62 & 39.33 & $\\mathbf{-2.83}${*} \\\\\n\\bottomrule\n\\end{tabular}\n\\end{table}\n\n\n\\subsection{Symbolic Confirmation Bias}\n\\label{sec:confirmation-bias}\n\nSelf-extracted constraints systematically confirm the model's majority answer regardless of correctness. On Mistral-7B FOLIO ($n = 204$), when SICA errs, 83.3\\% of extracted constraints support the incorrect majority answer---a bias ratio of $4.97\\times$ (Table~\\ref{tab:cross-model-bias}). Consequently, 94\\% of SICA errors overlap with SC errors. The MAX-SAT solver rejects only 3.4\\% of constraints on average, confirming degeneration (Eq.~\\ref{eq:degenerate}).\n\nThe causal chain is: the model generates an answer, then extracts symbolic constraints consistent with it. Constraints use trace-specific variable names (56.4\\% zero cross-answer variable overlap), so MAX-SAT retains nearly all and aggregation reduces to weighted majority voting. Confirmation bias generalizes across families: $4.97\\times$ (Mistral), $3.93\\times$ (Qwen-14B), and $2.88\\times$ (LLaMA), all exceeding $1.0$ (Table~\\ref{tab:cross-model-bias}). Bias intensifies along reasoning steps: late-step constraints (step $\\geq 7$) exhibit 27.2\\% conclusion leakage vs.\\ 22.0\\% for early steps (Fisher $p = 3 \\times 10^{-6}$); SICA restricted to late-only constraints degrades by $-10.3$\\,pp (Appendix~\\ref{app:error-categorization}).\n\n\n\\subsection{Process-Level Independence}\n\\label{sec:process-kappa}\n\nAnswer-level $\\kappa$ may underestimate the independence bottleneck: two traces can follow nearly identical reasoning yet reach different final answers, causing $\\kappa$ to treat them as disagreeing. We define process similarity as the mean pairwise cosine similarity of TF-IDF trace representations:\n\\begin{equation}\n\\label{eq:process-sim}\n\\text{ProcessSim}(q) = \\frac{2}{K(K{-}1)} \\sum_{i < j} \\cos(\\mathbf{v}_i^q, \\mathbf{v}_j^q),\n\\end{equation}\nwhere $\\mathbf{v}_i^q$ is the TF-IDF vector of trace $t_i$ for question $q$. On Mistral-7B FOLIO ($n = 204$), mean ProcessSim is $0.69$, far exceeding answer-level $\\kappa = 0.41$. The process--answer correlation is model-strength dependent: $r = -0.06$ ($p = 0.36$) on Mistral-7B, where answer diversity does not reflect reasoning diversity (Figure~\\ref{fig:process-kappa}), rising to $r = +0.24$ ($p < 0.001$) on Qwen2.5-14B. This pattern generalizes across four families (Table~\\ref{tab:cross-model-process}): the cosine--$\\kappa$ gap narrows from $0.23$ (Mistral) to $0.02$ (Qwen-14B), meaning answer-level $\\kappa$ already captures the bottleneck for strong models, while process-$\\kappa$ reveals hidden homogeneity in weaker models where answer diversity masks process convergence. In all families, interventions targeting answer diversity (temperature scaling, diverse prompting) cannot alter the underlying reasoning paths.\n\n\\begin{figure}[t]\n\\centering\n\\includegraphics[width=0.85\\columnwidth]{figures/fig3_process_kappa_scatter.pdf}\n\\vspace{-2mm}\n\\caption{Answer agreement vs.\\ CoT cosine similarity (Mistral-7B, FOLIO, $n{=}204$). Flat regression ($r = -0.06$, $p = 0.36$); stronger models show significant positive correlation ($r = +0.24$, Qwen-14B; Table~\\ref{tab:cross-model-process}).}\n\\label{fig:process-kappa}\n\\end{figure}\n\n\\subsection{Supporting Diagnostics}\n\\label{sec:diagnostics}\n\nFurther diagnostics---constraint ablation, discriminativeness, entropy decomposition, and hyperparameter sensitivity---confirm the mechanism (Appendices~\\ref{app:extraction}--\\ref{app:entropy}).\n\n\n\\subsection{Remediation Analysis}\n\\label{sec:remediation}\n\\label{sec:interventions}\n\nTwenty-five-plus remediation strategies all produce neutral or negative results. At the prompt level, nine strategies fail: contrastive prompting ($\\Delta = -1.1$\\,pp), cross-model extraction ($\\pm 1$\\,pp), multi-temperature ($\\Delta = 0.00$\\,pp; Appendix Table~\\ref{tab:remediation}). At the method level, ICG ($\\Delta \\approx 0$), Contrastive ($-9.31$\\,pp, $p < 0.01$), and Debate ($-18.63$/$-23.00$\\,pp, $p < 0.001$) all fail (Table~\\ref{tab:method_interventions}). Fifteen further experiments produce neutral-to-negative results (Appendix Table~\\ref{tab:additional_interventions}).\n\n\\paragraph{Independence-accuracy trade-off.} SC, Diverse-Prompt, and ICG cluster at $\\kappa \\approx 0.40$--$0.47$; Debate reaches $\\kappa = 0.15$ but collapses accuracy by $-18.6$\\,pp. All families cluster above $\\kappa = 0.40$, confirming a same-model ceiling: methods that increase independence destroy accuracy, and vice versa (Appendix Table~\\ref{tab:kappa_generalization}).",

       ```
       A natural hypothesis is that deterministic formal verification---where a Z3 solver provides logically guaranteed judgments---might bypass natural-language self-correction limitations by introducing a non-neural reasoning channel. We test this systematically and find the bottleneck shifts from the verifier to the extractor: self-extracted constraints inherit the model's bias regardless of verification formalism (\S\ref{sec:confirmation-bias}). More broadly, self-correction without external feedback degrades performance \citep{huang2024selfcorrect, stechly2025selfverification}; our quantitative diagnostics (BR, Eff-$K$, process-$\kappa$) provide reusable tools for predicting when any trace-aggregation method will fail.
       ```
     - Second batch (Patch 2): Added Weng/Madaan citation sentence in para 3:

        "content": "\\section{Analysis}\n\\label{sec:analysis}\n\nThis section tests the three predictions of the Symbolic Mirage hypothesis (\\S\\ref{sec:mirage}): trace correlation and $K_{\\text{eff}}$ collapse (\\S\\ref{sec:effective-k}), systematic confirmation bias with $\\text{BR} > 1$ across architectures (\\S\\ref{sec:confirmation-bias}), process-level independence (\\S\\ref{sec:process-kappa}), supporting diagnostics (\\S\\ref{sec:diagnostics}), and the inescapability of the mirage under twenty-plus same-model interventions (\\S\\ref{sec:remediation}).\n\n\\subsection{Condorcet Effective-K}\n\\label{sec:effective-k}\n\nTable~\\ref{tab:independence} reports Fleiss' $\\kappa$ and Effective-K (Eq.~\\ref{eq:effk}) across 15 conditions at $K = 12$. Among instruction-tuned models, $\\kappa$ ranges from 0.21 to 0.82, yielding $K_{\\text{eff}} = 1.20\\text{--}3.62$. The four Mistral-7B FOLIO seeds produce $\\kappa \\in [0.41, 0.51]$, yet $\\Delta$ ranges from $+3.43$ to $-0.49$\\,pp---seed-level constraint quality, not independence alone, appears to influence the variation. Two conditions are jointly necessary for positive $\\Delta$: sufficient $K_{\\text{eff}}$ ($\\leq 1.30$ never improves) and sufficient SC accuracy ($K_{\\text{eff}} = 3.62$ with SC $= 39\\%$ produces significant degradation). Cross-model validation confirms universality: all four families produce $\\kappa \\geq 0.41$ (Table~\\ref{tab:kappa_generalization}; Appendix~\\ref{app:cross-model-kappa}).\n\n\\begin{table}[t]\n\\centering\n\\small\n\\caption{Trace independence metrics ($K = 12$, sorted by $\\kappa$ descending). $\\kappa$: Fleiss' Kappa. $K_{\\text{eff}}$: Effective-K (Eq.~\\ref{eq:effk}). SC: Self-Consistency baseline. $\\Delta$: SICA $-$ SC (pp). {*}\\,$p < 0.05$; positive $\\Delta$ values do not reach significance. multi-T $= T \\in \\{0.3, 0.7, 1.2\\}$, 4 traces each.}\n\\label{tab:independence}\n\\begin{tabular}{@{}llccrr@{}}\n\\toprule\nModel & Condition & $\\kappa$ & $K_{\\text{eff}}$ & SC (\\%) & $\\Delta$ (pp) \\\\\n\\midrule\nQwen3 (think) & FOLIO & 0.91 & 1.09 & ${\\sim}$84 & ${\\sim}$0 \\\\\nQwen2.5-14B & LogiQA & 0.82 & 1.20 & 78.50 & $-$0.50 \\\\\nQwen3 & FOLIO & 0.77 & 1.26 & 84.80 & $-$0.49 \\\\\nQwen2.5-14B & FOLIO & 0.74 & 1.30 & 75.49 & $-$0.98 \\\\\nQwen2.5-7B & FOLIO & 0.66 & 1.46 & 75.98 & $+$0.49 \\\\\n\\addlinespace\nMistral-7B ($T{=}0.3$) & FOLIO & 0.53 & 1.77 & 58.33 & $-$1.47 \\\\\nMistral-7B & LogiQA & 0.51 & 1.81 & 51.00 & $+$0.50 \\\\\nMistral-7B (s456) & FOLIO & 0.51 & 1.82 & 55.88 & 0.00 \\\\\nMistral-7B & FOLIO & 0.50 & 1.84 & 55.88 & $+$3.43 \\\\\nMistral-7B (s123) & FOLIO & 0.49 & 1.88 & 54.41 & $+$2.94 \\\\\nMistral-7B (s789) & FOLIO & 0.41 & 2.17 & 54.41 & $-$0.49 \\\\\nMistral-7B ($T{=}0.5$) & FOLIO & 0.52 & 1.80 & 58.33 & $-$0.98 \\\\\nMistral-7B (multi-T) & FOLIO & 0.48 & 1.91 & 57.84 & 0.00 \\\\\nMistral-7B ($T{=}1.0$) & FOLIO & 0.48 & 1.91 & 55.39 & 0.00 \\\\\nMistral-7B & PW-D5 & 0.21 & 3.62 & 39.33 & $\\mathbf{-2.83}${*} \\\\\n\\bottomrule\n\\end{tabular}\n\\end{table}\n\n\n\\subsection{Symbolic Confirmation Bias}\n\\label{sec:confirmation-bias}\n\nSelf-extracted constraints systematically confirm the model's majority answer regardless of correctness. On Mistral-7B FOLIO ($n = 204$), when SICA errs, 83.3\\% of extracted constraints support the incorrect majority answer---a bias ratio of $4.97\\times$ (Table~\\ref{tab:cross-model-bias}). Consequently, 94\\% of SICA errors overlap with SC errors. The MAX-SAT solver rejects only 3.4\\% of constraints on average, confirming degeneration (Eq.~\\ref{eq:degenerate}).\n\nThe causal chain is: the model generates an answer, then extracts symbolic constraints consistent with it. Constraints use trace-specific variable names (56.4\\% zero cross-answer variable overlap), so MAX-SAT retains nearly all and aggregation reduces to weighted majority voting. Confirmation bias generalizes across families: $4.97\\times$ (Mistral), $3.93\\times$ (Qwen-14B), and $2.88\\times$ (LLaMA), all exceeding $1.0$ (Table~\\ref{tab:cross-model-bias}). Bias intensifies along reasoning steps: late-step constraints (step $\\geq 7$) exhibit 27.2\\% conclusion leakage vs.\\ 22.0\\% for early steps (Fisher $p = 3 \\times 10^{-6}$); SICA restricted to late-only constraints degrades by $-10.3$\\,pp (Appendix~\\ref{app:error-categorization}).\n\n\n\\subsection{Process-Level Independence}\n\\label{sec:process-kappa}\n\nAnswer-level $\\kappa$ may underestimate the independence bottleneck: two traces can follow nearly identical reasoning yet reach different final answers, causing $\\kappa$ to treat them as disagreeing. We define process similarity as the mean pairwise cosine similarity of TF-IDF trace representations:\n\\begin{equation}\n\\label{eq:process-sim}\n\\text{ProcessSim}(q) = \\frac{2}{K(K{-}1)} \\sum_{i < j} \\cos(\\mathbf{v}_i^q, \\mathbf{v}_j^q),\n\\end{equation}\nwhere $\\mathbf{v}_i^q$ is the TF-IDF vector of trace $t_i$ for question $q$. On Mistral-7B FOLIO ($n = 204$), mean ProcessSim is $0.69$, far exceeding answer-level $\\kappa = 0.41$. The process--answer correlation is model-strength dependent: $r = -0.06$ ($p = 0.36$) on Mistral-7B, where answer diversity does not reflect reasoning diversity (Figure~\\ref{fig:process-kappa}), rising to $r = +0.24$ ($p < 0.001$) on Qwen2.5-14B. This pattern generalizes across four families (Table~\\ref{tab:cross-model-process}): the cosine--$\\kappa$ gap narrows from $0.23$ (Mistral) to $0.02$ (Qwen-14B), meaning answer-level $\\kappa$ already captures the bottleneck for strong models, while process-$\\kappa$ reveals hidden homogeneity in weaker models where answer diversity masks process convergence. In all families, interventions targeting answer diversity (temperature scaling, diverse prompting) cannot alter the underlying reasoning paths.\n\n\n\\subsection{Supporting Diagnostics}\n\\label{sec:diagnostics}\n\nFurther diagnostics---constraint ablation, discriminativeness, entropy decomposition, and hyperparameter sensitivity---confirm the mechanism (Appendices~\\ref{app:extraction}--\\ref{app:entropy}).\n\n\n\\subsection{Remediation Analysis}\n\\label{sec:remediation}\n\\label{sec:interventions}\n\nTwenty-five-plus remediation strategies all produce neutral or negative results. At the prompt level, nine strategies fail: contrastive prompting ($\\Delta = -1.1$\\,pp), cross-model extraction ($\\pm 1$\\,pp), multi-temperature ($\\Delta = 0.00$\\,pp; Appendix Table~\\ref{tab:remediation}). At the method level, ICG ($\\Delta \\approx 0$), Contrastive ($-9.31$\\,pp, $p < 0.01$), and Debate ($-18.63$/$-23.00$\\,pp, $p < 0.001$) all fail (Table~\\ref{tab:method_interventions}). Fifteen further experiments produce neutral-to-negative results (Appendix Table~\\ref{tab:additional_interventions}).\n\n\\paragraph{Independence-accuracy trade-off.} SC, Diverse-Prompt, and ICG cluster at $\\kappa \\approx 0.40$--$0.47$; Debate reaches $\\kappa = 0.15$ but collapses accuracy by $-18.6$\\,pp. All families cluster above $\\kappa = 0.40$, confirming a same-model ceiling: methods that increase independence destroy accuracy, and vice versa (Appendix Table~\\ref{tab:kappa_generalization}; Figure~\\ref{fig:interventions-summary}).\n\n\\begin{figure}[t]\n\\centering\n\\includegraphics[width=0.85\\columnwidth]{figures/fig_app_interventions_summary.pdf}\n\\caption{Summary of 22+ intervention strategies grouped by category. All produce neutral-to-negative accuracy changes relative to Self-Consistency. The grey band indicates $\\pm 1$\\,pp noise zone.}\n\\label{fig:interventions-summary}\n\\end{figure}",
        "numLines": 79,
        "startLine": 1,
        "totalLines": 79
      }
    },

        "filePath": "/home/ubuntu/.agent-ml-research-idea_gen_0513_3/projects/symb_invariant_consensus/docs/paper/related_work.tex",
        "content": "\\section{Related Work}\n\\label{sec:related-work}\n\n\\paragraph{Self-Consistency and extensions.}\nSC selects the most frequent answer across chain-of-thought traces \\citep{wang2023sc}. Extensions include universal SC for free-form generation \\citep{chen2024usc}, confidence weighting \\citep{taubenfeld2025cisc}, diverse prompting with step-aware verification \\citep{li2023diverse}, program-based vote filtering \\citep{toh2024notallvotes}, and reasoning-aware filtering \\citep{wan2024rasc, kim2026reasc}. \\citet{loo2025reevaluating} show that SC gains plateau early. Our Condorcet analysis explains this: $\\kappa = 0.21$--$0.82$ compresses $K_{\\text{eff}}$ to 1.20--3.62 for $K = 12$ (\\S\\ref{sec:effective-k}).\n\n\\paragraph{Neuro-symbolic reasoning.}\nLogic-LM \\citep{pan2023logiclm} and LINC \\citep{olausson2023linc} translate problems into formal logic for external provers; LINC documents ${\\sim}$40\\% translation errors. Recent extensions---CLOVER \\citep{ryu2025clover}, DyLoS \\citep{xu2026dylos}, Aristotle \\citep{li2025aristotle}, FoVer \\citep{wang2025fover}, VERGE \\citep{singh2026verge}, LogicAgent \\citep{luo2025logicagent}---add compositional translation, solver selection, decomposition, trained verifiers, and formal refinement, each introducing capabilities beyond the reasoning model. \\citet{amonkar2025realitycheck} show that LLM formalization degrades with complexity; \\citet{chan2026position} argue that logical soundness is not a reliable criterion for neurosymbolic verification with LLMs. Our oracle controls confirm the mechanism is sound ($+29.90$/$+14.22$\\,pp FOLIO, $+11.50$\\,pp ProofWriter); the failure localizes to self-extraction quality (\\S\\ref{sec:oracle}).\n\n\\paragraph{Test-time compute and verification.}\nScaling test-time computation through additional traces \\citep{snell2025scaling} faces the same correlation challenge: same-model attempts collapse into correlated solutions. Reasoning models (o1, DeepSeek-R1 \\citep{guo2025deepseekr1}, QwQ \\citep{qwen2024qwq}) extend thinking time but remain subject to the independence bottleneck: QwQ-32B produces $\\kappa = 0.91$ and $C_0 = 2.6\\%$, suggesting that longer reasoning chains intensify homogenization rather than alleviating it. Trained verifiers offer an alternative \\citep{cobbe2021gsm8k, lightman2024prm, wang2024mathshepherd}; multi-agent debate refines answers through discussion \\citep{liang2023encouraging, du2023improving}. Answer-level self-verification \\citep{weng2023selfverification} and iterative self-refinement \\citep{madaan2023selfrefine} report gains using natural-language feedback, but neither employs formal symbolic verification. A natural hypothesis is that deterministic formal verification---where a Z3 solver provides logically guaranteed judgments---might bypass natural-language self-correction limitations by introducing a non-neural reasoning channel. We test this systematically and find the bottleneck shifts from the verifier to the extractor: self-extracted constraints inherit the model's bias regardless of verification formalism (\\S\\ref{sec:confirmation-bias}). More broadly, self-correction without external feedback degrades performance \\citep{huang2024selfcorrect, stechly2025selfverification}; our quantitative diagnostics (BR, Eff-$K$, process-$\\kappa$) provide reusable tools for predicting when any trace-aggregation method will fail.\n\n\\paragraph{CJT and ensemble diversity.}\nCJT \\citep{condorcet1785} guarantees convergence under independent voting; \\citet{ladha1992correlated} prove correlated voters reduce effective ensemble size; \\citet{lefort2024condorcet_llm} apply CJT to LLM classification. We extend this by quantifying \\emph{why} self-correction fails---same-model traces are correlated voters ($K_{\\text{eff}}$ collapses)---and showing this persists even under formal verification (Z3-checked symbolic constraints) and at the process level (\\S\\ref{sec:process-kappa}).\n\n\\paragraph{Positioning.}\nPrior self-correction studies \\citep{huang2024selfcorrect, stechly2025selfverification} establish qualitative negative results; we contribute reusable quantitative diagnostics that characterize the failure mode. Effective-$K$ (Eq.~\\ref{eq:effk}) measures how much independent information $K$ traces actually carry. The bias ratio ($\\text{BR}$; Eq.~\\ref{eq:mirage}) quantifies the degree to which self-extracted constraints post-hoc rationalize the model's answer rather than independently verify it, connecting symbolic extraction to CoT faithfulness concerns \\citep{turpin2024language}. Conclusion leakage rate ($C_D$) isolates answer-dependent constraint contamination at the step level. Together, these diagnostics show that formal Z3-checked verification inherits the same bias ($\\text{BR} = 4.97\\times$)---a finding invisible to prior work that examines only natural-language self-correction. The cross-architecture escape (\\S\\ref{sec:cross-arch}) yields a capability gap corollary: external verification helps only when the verifier's standalone accuracy exceeds the generator's SC baseline, providing a practical decision criterion absent from existing frameworks.",

        "startLine": 1,
        "totalLines": 17
      }
    },
